\rVert\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}(\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{b})(\omega,\cdot,\cdot,\xi)\rVert_{H^{s}_{\varphi,x}}\langle\xi\rangle^{\eta-(m+m^{\prime})}\leq_{\eta,s,m,m^{\prime}}|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s_{0},\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}\leq_{\eta,s,m,m^{\prime}}|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s_{0},\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s,\eta},

(2.39)

This immediately implies (
2.38
) by taking the supremum in
\omega
. For the Lipschitz dependence in (
2.37
), we have (denoting
\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{a}:=\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{1})-\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{2})
), that

\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{1})\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{1})-\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{2})\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{2})=(\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{a})\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{1})+\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{2})\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{b}.

Hence,

\frac{\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{1})\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{1})-\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{2})\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{2})}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}=\frac{\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{a}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{1})+\mathfrak{a}_{2}(\omega_{2})\frac{\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{b}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}.

Again using (
2.39
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{1})\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{1})-\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{2})\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{2})\right|_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}

\displaystyle\quad\leq_{\eta,s,m,m^{\prime}}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s_{0},\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}+|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{lip}}_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{lip}}_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s,\eta}

\displaystyle\quad\leq(|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s,\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s,\eta})(|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{lip}}_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta})+(|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s_{0},\eta})(|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{lip}}_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}+|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}).

Then, the result follows by multiplying by
\gamma
on both sides and taking the supremum in
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
.
∎

Remark 2.12
.

In case
\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{a}(\omega,\xi)\in\mathcal{S}^{m}
and
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{b}(\omega,\xi)\in\mathcal{S}^{m^{\prime}}
are Fourier multipliers, that is, they do not depend on
x,\varphi
, then (
2.28
) shows that

Op^{W}(\mathfrak{a})\circ Op^{W}(\mathfrak{b})=Op^{W}(\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{b}).

Using the norms in (
2.11
) and (
2.7
), it follows straightforwardly from Lemma
2.11
that

|\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,H^{0}_{x},\eta}\leq_{\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,H^{0}_{x},\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,H^{0}_{x},\eta}.

Now, we estimate symbols obtained from a composition of two symbols. In view of Lemma
2.7
, we prove the following lemma:

Lemma 2.13
.

Given
\omega,\varphi
dependent symbols
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{S}^{m}
,
\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{S}^{m^{\prime}}
for some
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, we define
\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
be such that

\widehat{\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}(\omega,\varphi,k,\xi):=\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=k}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}(\omega,\varphi,j_{1},\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2})\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}(\omega,\varphi,j_{2},\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}).

Then, for each
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists
\mu=\mu(m,m^{\prime},\eta)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|\tau_{1}|,|\tau_{2}|\leq 1}|\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}|^{\sup}_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},s,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta}.

(2.40)

And,

\displaystyle\sup_{|\tau_{1}|,|\tau_{2}|\leq 1}

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}\leq_{\nu,m,m^{\prime},s,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta}\text{ for $|\gamma|\leq 1$}.

(2.41)

Proof.

Using the notation in (
2.36
) and recalling the norm
|\cdot|_{m,s,\eta}
in (
2.34
), it is clear that for each fixed
\omega\in\Omega
,

\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z},\ l\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}}(\langle j\rangle+\langle l\rangle)^{2s}|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l,j,\xi)|^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(\eta-m)}\leq_{m,\eta,s}|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|^{2}_{m,s,\eta}.

Therefore for each fixed
(l,j,\xi)
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l,j,\xi)|\leq_{m,s,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s,\eta}\frac{\langle\xi\rangle^{m-\eta}}{(\langle j\rangle+\langle l\rangle)^{s}},\text{ for each $s\geq s_{0}$}.

(2.42)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l,j,\xi)|\leq_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s,\eta_{0}}\frac{\langle\xi\rangle^{m^{\prime}-\eta}}{(\langle j\rangle+\langle l\rangle)^{s}},\text{ for each $s\geq s_{0}$}.

(2.43)

Then we can write each Fourier mode of
\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
as

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l,k,\xi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}\binom{\eta}{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}\end{subarray}}\partial_{\xi}^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{1},j_{1},\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2})\partial_{\xi}^{\eta-n}\widehat{b}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{2},j_{2},\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}).

Using that
(\langle k\rangle+\langle l\rangle)^{2s}\leq_{s}(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{2s}+(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{2s}
for
j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\ l_{1}+l_{2}=l
, we have

\displaystyle(\langle k\rangle

\displaystyle+\langle l\rangle)^{2s}|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l,k,\xi)|^{2}

(2.44)

\displaystyle\leq_{s,\eta,n}\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}\left(\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\end{subarray}}(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{s}|(\partial_{\xi})^{\eta-n}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{1},j_{1},\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2})||(\partial_{\xi})^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{2},j_{2},\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1})|\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\ +\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\end{subarray}}(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{s}|(\partial_{\xi})^{\eta-n}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{1},j_{1},\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2})||(\partial_{\xi})^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{2},j_{2},\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1})|\right)^{2}\right).

Using (
2.42
) and (
2.43
), we have that for some
\mu=\mu(m,m^{\prime},\eta)\geq 0
, which will be chosen later,

\displaystyle|(\partial_{\xi})^{\eta-n}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{1},j_{1},\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2})||(\partial_{\xi})^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{2},j_{2},\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta,s}|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s+\mu,\eta}\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}}{(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{s+\mu}}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta_{0}}\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}}{(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{s_{0}+\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta}\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}}{(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{s+\mu}(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{s_{0}+\mu}}.

(2.45)

Similarly, the same quantity can be estimated as

\displaystyle|(\partial_{\xi})^{\eta-n}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{1},j_{1},\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2})||(\partial_{\xi})^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l_{2},j_{2},\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta,s}|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta}\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}}{(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{s_{0}+\mu}(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{s+\mu}}.

(2.46)

Plugging these two estimates into (
2.44
) (Plugging (
2.45
) into the first summation and plugging (
2.2.1.4
) into the second summation into (
2.44
)), we obtain

\displaystyle(\langle k\rangle

\displaystyle+\langle l\rangle)^{2s}|(\partial_{\xi})^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l,k,\xi)|^{2}

\displaystyle\ \leq_{m,m^{\prime},s,\eta}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta}\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}}{(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{\mu}(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{s_{0}+\mu}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\ +\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta}\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}}{(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{s_{0}+\mu}(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{\mu}}\right)^{2}

Using the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we get (using that
\sum_{j,l}\left(\frac{1}{\langle j\rangle+\langle l\rangle}\right)^{s_{0}}<_{s_{0}}1
, thanks to (
2.5
)),

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z},\ l\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}}(\langle k\rangle

\displaystyle+\langle l\rangle)^{2s}|(\partial_{\xi})^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}^{\varphi,x}(\omega,l,k,\xi)|^{2}

(2.47)

\displaystyle\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta,s}(|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s+\mu,\eta_{0}}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta})^{2}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},\ l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}}\left(\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}}{(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{\mu}(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{\mu}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(|\mathfrak{a}(\omega)|_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}(\omega)|_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta}\right)^{2}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},\ l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}}\left(\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}}{(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{\mu}(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{\mu}}\right)^{2}.

Now, we estimate the summations in the above estimate. Since
|\tau_{1}|,|\tau_{2}|\leq 1
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta_{0}}\langle\xi\rangle^{m+m^{\prime}-\eta},\text{ for $|j_{1}|,|j_{2}|\leq\frac{|\xi|}{2}$}.

(2.48)

If
|j_{1}|>\frac{|\xi|}{2}
or
|j_{2}|>\frac{|\xi|}{2}
, then one can choose large enough
\mu
depending only on
m,m^{\prime},\eta
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},\\
\text{$|j_{1}|\geq\frac{|\xi|}{2}$ or $|j_{2}|\geq\frac{|\xi|}{2}$},\\
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\langle\xi+\tau_{1}j_{2}\rangle^{m-(\eta-n)}\langle\xi+\tau_{2}j_{1}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-n}}{(\langle j_{1}\rangle+\langle l_{1}\rangle)^{\mu}(\langle j_{2}\rangle+\langle l_{2}\rangle)^{\mu}}\right)^{2}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(m+m^{\prime}-\eta)}.

Combining this with (
2.48
) and taking the supremum in
\omega
, the estimate in (
2.47
) yields (
2.40
),
Now we turn to (
2.41
). it is clear that (denoting
\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{a}:=\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{1})-\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{2})
),

\frac{\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}=\frac{\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{a}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{1})+\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{2})\frac{\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{b}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}.

Applying (
2.40
) to
\frac{\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{a}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}\mathfrak{b}(\omega_{1})
and
\mathfrak{a}(\omega_{2})\frac{\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{b}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}
, we obtain that for each
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
,

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}|_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta_{0}}

(2.49)

\displaystyle\leq_{\nu,m,m^{\prime},s,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m,s+\mu,\eta}

\displaystyle+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s+\mu,\eta}

\displaystyle\leq(|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s+\mu,\eta})(|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{lip}}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta})

\displaystyle+(|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{lip}}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\sup}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta})(|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{lip}}_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{b}|^{\sup}_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta}).

Noting that
|\gamma|<1
and taking the supremum in
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
, we get

\gamma|\mathfrak{c}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}|^{\text{lip}}_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta}.

Combining this with (
2.40
) and recalling the definition of the norm in (
2.7
), the desired estimate (
2.41
) follows.
∎

Lemma 2.14
.

Given
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{S}^{m}
,
\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{S}^{m^{\prime}}
for some
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\sigma^{1}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}},\sigma^{2}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}
be the symbols given in (
2.24
).
Then, we have
\sigma_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}^{1}\in\mathcal{S}^{m+m^{\prime}-2},\sigma^{2}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}\in\mathcal{S}^{m+m^{\prime}-3}
. Furthermore, for each
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exist
\mu=\mu({m,m^{\prime},\eta})
and an absolute constant
N>0
, such that

\displaystyle|\sigma^{1}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}|_{m+m^{\prime}-2,s,\eta}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq_{m,m^{\prime},s,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}+|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)},

\displaystyle|\sigma^{2}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}|_{m+m^{\prime}-3,s,\eta}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq_{m,m^{\prime},s,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}+|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}.

Consequently,
r_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}\in\mathcal{S}^{m+m^{\prime}-2}
in (
2.33
) satisfies

|r_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}|_{m+m^{\prime}-2,s,\eta}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},s,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}+|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}

Proof.

It follows straightforwardly from expressions for
\sigma^{1}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}},\sigma^{2}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}
in Lemma
2.7
and the estimates in Lemma
2.11
, and Lemma
2.13
.
∎

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{S}^{m}
and
\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{S}^{m^{\prime}}
for some
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
. For each
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exist
\mu=\mu(m,m^{\prime},\eta)
and an absolute constant
N>0
such that the followings hold true:

(1)

For
\mathfrak{c}
such that
Op^{W}(\mathfrak{a})\circ Op^{W}(\mathfrak{b})=Op^{W}(\mathfrak{c})
, we have
\mathfrak{c}\in S^{m+m^{\prime}}
and for all
s\geq s_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{c}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta,s}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s+\mu,\eta+N}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta+N}.

(2)

For
\mathfrak{c}
such that
[Op^{W}(\mathfrak{a}),Op^{W}(\mathfrak{b})]=Op^{W}(\mathfrak{c})
, we have
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{S}^{m+m^{\prime}-1}
and for all
s\geq s_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{c}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m+m^{\prime}-1,s,\eta}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta,s}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s+\mu,\eta+N}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta+N}.

(3)

For
\mathfrak{c}
such that
[Op^{W}(\mathfrak{a}),Op^{W}(\mathfrak{b})]_{x}=Op^{W}(\mathfrak{c})
, we have
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{S}^{m+m^{\prime}}
and for all
s\geq s_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{c}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},\eta,s}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s+\mu,\eta+N}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s_{0}+\mu,\eta+N}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s+\mu,\eta+N}.

Proof.

The result follows from Lemma
2.13
, Lemma
2.7
, Lemma
2.11
, Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
2.14
.
∎

For
(1)
of Lemma
2.15
, we will obtain a finer estimate in case that
\mathfrak{a}
or
\mathfrak{b}
is supported on a finite number of Fourier modes:

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{S}^{m}
and
\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{S}^{m^{\prime}}
. Assume that
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{b}(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)
is supported on a finite number of Fourier modes, then we can estimate the symbol of the composition of
Op(\mathfrak{a})
and
Op(\mathfrak{b})
without any loss of derivatives. More precisely, if

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}(\omega,l,k,\xi)=0,\text{ if $|l|+|k|\geq N$ for some $N\geq 0$.}

(2.50)

then, for a symbol
\mathfrak{c}
such that
Op(\mathfrak{a})\circ Op(\mathfrak{b})=Op(\mathfrak{c})
, we have that

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{c}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}\leq_{m,m^{\prime},s,\eta,N}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m,s_{0},\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{m^{\prime},s,\eta},\text{ for all $s\geq s_{0}$.}

(2.51)

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
2.13
. Using (
2.23
), we have (omitting
\omega
dependence for simplicity),

\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{c}}^{\varphi,x}(l,k,\xi)=\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\ l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta-n}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{1},j_{1},\xi+{j_{2}})\partial_{\xi}^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{2},j_{2},\xi).

Hence,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\widehat{\mathfrak{c}}^{\varphi,x}(l,k,\xi)|^{2}(\langle l\rangle+\langle k\rangle)^{2s}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}\left|\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\ l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta-n}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{1},j_{1},\xi+{j_{2}})\partial_{\xi}^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{2},j_{2},\xi)\right|^{2}(\langle l\rangle+\langle k\rangle)^{2s}

\displaystyle\leq_{s,\eta}\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}\left|\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\ l_{1}+l_{2}=l}(\langle l_{1}\rangle+\langle j_{1}\rangle)^{s}|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta-n}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{1},j_{1},\xi+{j_{2}})||\partial_{\xi}^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{2},j_{2},\xi)|\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\ +\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}\left|\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\ l_{1}+l_{2}=l}|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta-n}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{1},j_{1},\xi+{j_{2}})|(\langle l_{2}\rangle+\langle j_{2}\rangle)^{s}|\partial_{\xi}^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{2},j_{2},\xi)|\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=A_{1}+A_{2}.

(2.52)

For
A_{1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu},k\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}\sum_{k,l}\left|\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=k,\ l_{1}+l_{2}=l}(\langle l_{1}\rangle+\langle j_{1}\rangle)^{s}|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta-n}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{1},j_{1},\xi+{j_{2}})|(\langle l_{2}\rangle+\langle j_{2}\rangle)^{s_{0}}|\partial_{\xi}^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{b}}^{\varphi,x}(l_{2},j_{2},\xi)|(\langle l_{2}\rangle+\langle j_{2}\rangle)^{-s_{0}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq_{s_{0}}\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},\ l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}}\left((\langle l_{1}\rangle+\langle j_{1}\rangle)^{s}|\widehat{\partial_{\xi}^{\eta-n}\mathfrak{a}}(l_{1},j_{1},\xi+{j_{2}})|(\langle l_{2}\rangle+\langle j_{2}\rangle)^{s_{0}}|\widehat{\partial_{\xi}^{n}\mathfrak{b}}(l_{2},j_{2},\xi)|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}\sum_{l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\rVert\partial_{\xi}^{\eta-n}\mathfrak{a}(\cdot,\cdot,\xi+j_{2})\rVert_{s}^{2}\left((\langle l_{2}\rangle+\langle j_{2}\rangle)^{s_{0}}|\widehat{\partial_{\xi}^{n}\mathfrak{b}}(l_{2},j_{2},\xi)|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{xxxxx12323sd}}{\leq}\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}\sum_{|l_{2}|+|j_{2}|\leq N}|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s,\eta}^{2}\langle\xi+j_{2}\rangle^{2(m-(\eta-n))}\left((\langle l_{2}\rangle+\langle j_{2}\rangle)^{s_{0}}|\widehat{\partial_{\xi}^{n}\mathfrak{b}}(l_{2},j_{2},\xi)|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq_{N,m,\eta}\sum_{n=0}^{\eta}C_{n}|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s,\eta}^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(m-(\eta-n))}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(m^{\prime}-n)}

\displaystyle\leq_{\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{2}_{m,s,\eta_{0}}|\mathfrak{b}|^{2}_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(m+m^{\prime}-\eta)}.

(2.53)

where the second inequality follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the fourth and fifth inequalities follow from the definition of the norm
|\cdot|_{m,s,\eta}
.
The same computations give us that

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu},k\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{2}\leq_{s,N,\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|^{2}_{m,s_{0},\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|^{2}_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(m+m^{\prime}-\eta)}.

Plugging this and (
2.53
) into (
2.52
),

\rVert\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\mathfrak{c}(\cdot,\cdot,\xi)\rVert_{s}^{2}\leq_{s,N,m,m^{\prime},\eta}\left(|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s_{0},\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}\right)^{2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(m+m^{\prime}-\eta)},

which implies

|\mathfrak{c}|_{m+m^{\prime},s,\eta}\leq_{s,N,m,m^{\prime},\eta}|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s,\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s_{0},\eta}+|\mathfrak{a}|_{m,s_{0},\eta}|\mathfrak{b}|_{m^{\prime},s,\eta}.

The Lipschitz dependence on
\omega
can be proved following the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
2.13
(especially following the computations in (
2.49
)). Then, (
2.51
) follows immediately.
∎

2.2.2
Lipschitz-tame operators

We recall from
[
16
, Section 2]
,
[
52
, Section 2]
the notion of the Lipschitz-tame estimates of linear operators.

Definition 2.17

(
\mu
-tame operators)
.

For
\mu\geq 0
and
\mathtt{S}\gg s_{0}
, a linear operator
\mathcal{A}:C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}\mapsto C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}
is said to be
\mu
-tame up to
\mathtt{S}
, if there exists a non-decreasing function
[s_{0},\mathtt{S}]\ni s\mapsto\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)\geq 0
, such that

\displaystyle\rVert\mathcal{A}h\rVert_{s}\leq\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)\rVert h\rVert_{s_{0}+\mu}+\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s_{0})\rVert h\rVert_{s+\mu},\text{ for all $h\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}$.}

\mathtt{S}
can be possibly chosen to be
\infty
, in which case, we simply say that the operator
\mathcal{A}
is
\mu
-tame.

We also consider Lipschitz dependence of the operators on the frequency
\omega\in\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
. To estimate such operators, we recall the following definition:

Definition 2.18

(Lip-
\mu
-tame operators)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}(\omega):C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}\mapsto C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}
be a
\omega
-dependent linear operator, for
\omega\in\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
. We denote

\Delta_{12}\mathcal{A}:=\frac{\mathcal{A}(\omega_{1})-\mathcal{A}(\omega_{2})}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}.

For
\mu\geq 0
,
\mathtt{S}\gg s_{0}
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
, the operator
\mathcal{A}
is said to be Lip-
\mu
-tame up to
\mathtt{S}
, if there exists a non-decreasing function
[s_{0},\mathtt{S}]\ni s\mapsto\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)\geq 0
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in\Omega}\rVert\mathcal{A}(\omega)h\rVert_{s}+\gamma\sup_{\omega_{1}\neq\omega_{2}\in\Omega}\rVert\Delta_{12}\mathcal{A}h\rVert_{s}\leq\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)\rVert h\rVert_{s_{0}+\mu}+\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s_{0})\rVert h\rVert_{s+\mu},\text{ for all $h\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}$.}

\mathtt{S}
can be possibly chosen to be
\infty
, in which case, we simply say that the operator
\mathcal{A}
is Lip-
\mu
-tame.

2.2.2.1
Majorant operators and modulo-tame operators

We recall the notion of majorant functions and majorant operators:

Definition 2.19

(Majorant functions/operators)
.

Given a function
u\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}
, we define its majorant as

\displaystyle\underline{u}(\varphi,x):=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu},\ j\in\mathbb{Z}}|u_{j}(l)|e^{\text{i}(l\cdot\varphi+jx)}.

(2.54)

Given a linear operator
\mathcal{A}
, we define its majorant as

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{A}}h:=\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},\ l,l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}}|\mathcal{A}_{j_{2}}^{j_{1}}(l-l^{\prime})|h_{j_{1}}(l^{\prime})e^{\text{i}(l\cdot\varphi+j_{2}x)}.

(2.55)

Using the notion of majorant operators, we define modulo-tame operators:

Definition 2.20

(
\mu
-modulo tame operators)
.

For
\mu\geq 0
and
\mathtt{S}\gg s_{0}
, a linear operator
\mathcal{A}:C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}\mapsto C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}
is said to be
\mu
-modulo tame up to
\mathtt{S}
, if there exists a non-decreasing function
[s_{0},\mathtt{S}]\ni s\mapsto\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)\geq 0
, such that

\displaystyle\rVert\underline{\mathcal{A}}h\rVert_{s}\leq\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)\rVert h\rVert_{s_{0}+\mu}+\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s_{0})\rVert h\rVert_{s+\mu},\text{ for all $h\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}$.}

\mathtt{S}
can be possibly chosen to be
\infty
, in which case, we simply say that the operator
\mathcal{A}
is
\mu
-tame.

Remark 2.21
.

Clearly, we have that

\displaystyle\rVert\underline{u}\rVert_{s}=\rVert u\rVert_{s},

(2.56)

while we cannot have
\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mu,s)\leq\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)
in general. In Section
10
, we will estimate tame constants of
[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]
for sufficiently large vector
\vec{b}
, as well as
\mathcal{A}
, to bound the modulo tame constant by tame constant (see Lemma
2.27
).

Definition 2.22

(Lip-
\mu
-modulo tame operators)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}(\omega):C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}\mapsto C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}
be a
\omega
-dependent linear operator, for
\omega\in\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
. For
\mu\geq 0
,
\mathtt{S}\gg s_{0}
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
, the operator
\mathcal{A}
is said to be Lip-
\mu
-modulo tame up to
\mathtt{S}
, if there exists a non-decreasing function
[s_{0},\mathtt{S}]\ni s\mapsto\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)\geq 0
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in\Omega}\rVert\underline{\mathcal{A}(\omega)}h\rVert_{s}+\gamma\sup_{\omega_{1}\neq\omega_{2}\in\Omega}\rVert\underline{\Delta_{12}\mathcal{A}}h\rVert_{s}\leq\mathfrak{M}^{{}^{\sharp},\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s)\rVert h\rVert_{s_{0}+\mu}+\mathfrak{M}^{{}^{\sharp},\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mu,s_{0})\rVert h\rVert_{s+\mu},\text{ for all $h\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}$.}

\mathtt{S}
can be possibly chosen to be
\infty
, in which case, we simply say that the operator
\mathcal{A}
is Lip-
\mu
-modulo tame.

2.2.2.2
Properties of the tame operators

In what follows, we list useful properties of the tame operators. The proofs can be found in
[
16
, Section 2]
and
[
52
, Section 2]
.

We use the following notations: For
\varphi\in\mathbb{T}^{\nu}
, and
\vec{b}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\nu}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu}
, we denote

\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}:=\partial_{\varphi_{1}}^{\vec{b}_{1}}\ldots\partial_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{\vec{b}_{\nu}},\quad l^{\vec{b}}:=\Pi_{i=1}^{\nu}(l_{i})^{\vec{b}_{i}},\text{ where $l=(l_{1},\ldots,l_{\nu}),\ \vec{b}=(\vec{b}_{1},\ldots,\vec{b}_{\nu})$.}

Given
N\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and a linear operator
\mathcal{A}
, let us denote by
\Pi_{\leq N}\mathcal{A}
, the operator defined as

\displaystyle(\Pi_{N}\mathcal{A})^{j_{1}}_{j_{2}}(l):=\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}^{j_{1}}_{j_{2}}(l),&\text{ if $|l|\leq N$},\\
0,&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}

(2.57)

where
\mathcal{A}^{j_{1}}_{j_{2}}(l)
is as in (
2.9
), and we denote
\Pi_{>N}:=I-\Pi_{\leq N}
.

Lemma 2.23
.

[
54
, Lemma A.1]
[
16
, Lemma 2.25]
Let
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}
be Lip-
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}
-tame and Lip-
\mu_{\mathcal{B}}
-tame operators up to
\mathtt{S}
. Then,
\mathcal{A}\circ\mathcal{B}
is Lip-
\left(\mu_{A}+\mu_{B}\right)
-tame operator up to
\mathtt{S}
with tame constant

\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}\circ\mathcal{B}}^{\gamma}(\mu_{A}+\mu_{B},s)\leq\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\gamma}(\mu_{A},s)\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\gamma}(\mu_{\mathcal{B}},s_{0}+\mu_{A})+\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\gamma}(\mu_{A},s_{0})\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\gamma}(\mu_{B},s_{0}+\mu_{A})

If
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}=\mu_{\mathcal{B}}=0
, then we have that for
\vec{b}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\nu}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B})}(0,s)

\displaystyle\leq C(\vec{b})\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{B}}(0,s_{0})+\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s_{0})\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{B}}(0,s)\right.

(2.58)

\displaystyle\left.\ +\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s)\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{B})}(0,s_{0})+\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s_{0})\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{B})}(0,s)\right)

Lemma 2.24
.

[
54
, Lemma A.2]

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a Lip-
\mu
-tame operator up to
\mathtt{S}
for some
\mu\geq 0
and let
h=h(\omega)
be an
\omega
-dependent function in
C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}
. Then,

\displaystyle\rVert\mathcal{A}h\rVert_{s}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}\leq_{s}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\gamma}(\mu,s)\rVert h\rVert_{s_{0}+\mu}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}+\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\gamma}(\mu,s_{0})\rVert h\rVert_{s+\mu}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}.

Lemma 2.25

(Pseudo differential operator)
.

[
16
, Lemma 2.21]
Let
\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{a}(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)\in\mathcal{S}^{0}
be a symbol that depends on
\omega,\varphi
. For
s\geq s_{0}
,
\mathcal{A}:=Op^{W}(\mathfrak{a})
is a Lip-
0
-tame operator with tame constant

\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s)\leq_{s}|\mathfrak{a}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{0,s,0}.

Lemma 2.26
.

[
16
, Lemma 2.27, 2.28]

For
\vec{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\nu}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{\Pi_{>N}\mathcal{A}}(0,s)\leq N^{-|\mathtt{b}|}\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}\mathcal{A}}(0,s),\quad\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{\Pi_{>N}\mathcal{A}}(0,s)\leq\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s),

(2.59)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\Pi_{>N}\mathcal{A}}(0,s)\leq N^{-|\mathtt{b}|}\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}\mathcal{A}}(0,s),\quad\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\Pi_{>N}\mathcal{A}}(0,s)\leq\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s).

(2.60)

Lemma 2.27
.

(Lipschitz-tame to Lipschitz-modulo-tame)
Let
\mathtt{b}_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
be fixed. For each
\vec{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\nu}
such that
|\vec{\mathtt{b}}|\leq s_{0}+\mathtt{b}_{0}
, assume that
\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})
and
[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]
are Lip-
0
-tame operators up to
\mathtt{S}
with tame constants
\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s),\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]}(0,s)
for
s\in[s_{0},\mathtt{S}]
. Then, for all
\vec{b}\in\mathbb{N}^{\nu}
such that
|\vec{b}|\leq\mathtt{b}_{0}
,
\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A})
is Lipschitz-0-modulo tame up to
\mathtt{S}
and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)\leq_{s_{0},\mathtt{b}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\nu},\\
|\vec{\mathtt{b}}|\leq s_{0}+\mathtt{b}_{0}\end{subarray}}\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)+\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]}(0,s)\right).

(2.61)

Proof.

To simplify the notation, let us denote for fixed
\omega,\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}(\omega),&\text{ if $k=0$,}\\
\gamma\frac{\mathcal{A}(\omega_{1})-\mathcal{A}(\omega_{2})}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|},&\text{ if $k=1$.}\end{cases}

(2.62)

First, we test the operator
\mathcal{A}
with a monomial
h(\varphi,x):=e^{\text{i}(l^{\prime}\cdot\varphi+j^{\prime}x)}
, then it follows from the definition of
\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)
that

\displaystyle\rVert\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})h\rVert_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)\right)^{2}\rVert h\rVert_{s_{0}}^{2}+\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}\rVert h\rVert_{s}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=C\left(\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)\right)^{2}\langle l^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s_{0}}+\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}\langle l^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s}\right),

Using the matrix representation of a linear operator in (
2.9
), we have

\left(\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})\right)^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime})=\text{i}^{|\vec{\mathtt{b}}|}(l-l^{\prime})^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A})^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime}).

Therefore, the above inequality for
\rVert\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})h\rVert_{s}^{2}
gives us

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu+1}}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s}|(l-l^{\prime})^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}|^{2}\left|\left(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A}\right)^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime})\right|^{2}\leq C\left(\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)\right)^{2}\langle l^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s_{0}}+\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}\langle l^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s}\right).

(2.63)

Similarly, using the matrix representation,

([\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}])^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime})=\text{i}^{|\vec{\mathtt{b}}|+1}(l-l^{\prime})^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(j-j^{\prime})(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A})^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime}),

and using that
[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]
is also Lip-
0
-tame, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu+1}}

\displaystyle\langle l,j\rangle^{2s}|(l-l^{\prime})^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}|^{2}|j-j^{\prime}|^{2}\left|\left(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A}\right)^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]}(0,s)\right)^{2}\langle l^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s_{0}}+\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}\langle l^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s}\right).

(2.64)

Now, we recall the majorant operator from (
2.55
) and see that for all
h\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi,x}
,

\displaystyle\rVert\underline{\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A}})h\rVert_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu+1}}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s}\left(\sum_{(l^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{\nu+1}}|(\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A}))^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime})|h_{j^{\prime}}(l^{\prime})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu+1}}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s}\left(\sum_{(l^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{\nu+1}}|(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A})^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime})||(l-l^{\prime})^{\vec{b}}|\langle l-l^{\prime}\rangle^{s_{0}}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle|h_{j^{\prime}}(l^{\prime})|\frac{1}{\langle l-l^{\prime}\rangle^{s_{0}}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq_{s_{0}}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu+1}}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s}\sum_{(l^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{\nu+1}}|(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A})^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime})|^{2}\langle l-l^{\prime}\rangle^{2(s_{0}+|\vec{b}|)}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle^{2}|h_{j^{\prime}}(l^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(l^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu+1}}|h_{j^{\prime}}(l^{\prime})|^{2}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{T}^{\nu+1}}|(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A})^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l-l^{\prime})|^{2}\langle l-l^{\prime}\rangle^{2(s_{0}+|\vec{b}|)}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle^{2}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s},

(2.65)

where the first inequality follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with (
2.5
). Since
|\vec{b}|\leq\mathtt{b}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle l-l^{\prime}\rangle^{2(s_{0}+|\vec{b}|)}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq_{s_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\nu},\\
|\vec{\mathtt{b}}|\leq s_{0}+\mathtt{b}_{0}\end{subarray}}\left(|(l-l^{\prime})^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}|^{2}+|(l-l^{\prime})^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}|^{2}|j-j^{\prime}|^{2}\right),

therefore, (
2.63
), (
2.64
) and (
2.65
) give us that

\displaystyle\rVert\underline{\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A}})h\rVert_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq_{s_{0},\mathtt{b}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\nu},\\
|\vec{\mathtt{b}}|\leq s_{0}+\mathtt{b}_{0}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{(l^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{\nu+1}}|h_{j^{\prime}}(l^{\prime})|^{2}\left(\langle l^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s_{0}}\left(\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)\right)^{2}+\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]}(0,s)\right)^{2}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\langle l^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s}\left(\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}+\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\nu},\\
|\vec{\mathtt{b}}|\leq s_{0}+\mathtt{b}_{0}\end{subarray}}\left(\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)\right)^{2}+\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]}(0,s)\right)^{2}\right)\rVert h\rVert_{s_{0}}

\displaystyle\ +\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\nu},\\
|\vec{\mathtt{b}}|\leq s_{0}+\mathtt{b}_{0}\end{subarray}}\left(\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}+\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{[\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{\mathtt{b}}}(\mathcal{A}),\partial_{x}]}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}\right)\rVert h\rVert_{s}.

Recalling the definition of the Lip-
0
-modulo tame operator in Definition
2.22
, we obtain (
2.61
).
∎

Lemma 2.28
.

[
16
, Lemma 2.26]

Let
\vec{b}\in\mathbb{N}^{\nu}_{0}
and
\Phi:=I+\mathcal{A}
where
\mathcal{A},\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A})
are Lip-
0
-modulo tame operators up to
\mathtt{S}
with modulo-tame constants
\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s),\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)
. Then, there exist constants
C=C(|\vec{b}|)
and
\delta=\delta(|\vec{b}|)>0
such that if
\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s_{0})\leq\delta
, then
\Phi
is invertible and
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}:=\Phi^{-1}-I
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}(0,s)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s),

(2.66)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}})}(0,s)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s)+\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\partial_{\varphi}^{\vec{b}}(\mathcal{A})}(0,s_{0})\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s)\right),\text{ for $s\in[s_{0},\mathtt{S}]$.}

Furthermore, the same statement holds true, replacing the Lip-
0
-modulo-tame constant by the
0
-modulo tame constant.

Proof.

The estimates (
2.66
) can be proved in the same way as in
[
16
, Lemma 2.26]
, just replacing the
\mathcal{D}^{k_{0}}
-
0
-modulo-tame constant by the Lip-
0
-tame constant. To obtain the result for the
0
-modulo-tame estimate, we can simply apply the estimates (
2.66
) for an operator that does not depend on
\omega
, noting that by definition,
\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s)=\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s)
, if
\mathcal{A}
is independent of
\omega
.
∎

Lemma 2.29
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a Lip-
0
-modulo-tame operator up to
\mathtt{S}
, with tame constant
\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s)
. We define

r(\omega,j):=\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}^{j}_{j}(0),&\text{ for $j\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}$,}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then, it holds that

|r|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{0,0,0}:=\sup_{\omega,\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\Omega,\ j\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}|r(\omega,j)|+\gamma\frac{|r(\omega_{1},j)-r(\omega_{2},j)|}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|}\leq_{s_{0}}\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s_{0}).

Proof.

Using the notation (
2.62
), let us denote

\Delta^{k}_{12}r(j):=\begin{cases}r(\omega,j),&\text{ if $k=0$,}\\
\gamma\frac{r(\omega_{1},j)-r(\omega_{2},j)}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|},&\text{ if $k=1$},\end{cases}

so that
\Delta^{k}_{12}r(j)=\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A}^{j}_{j}
. Plugging
l^{\prime}=0,\vec{\mathtt{b}}=0,s=s_{0}
in (
2.63
), we have

\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu+1}}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s_{0}}\left|\left(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A}\right)^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l)\right|^{2}\leq_{s_{0}}\left(\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s_{0})\right)^{2}\langle j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s_{0}},

while we obviously have

\langle j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s_{0}}\left|\left(\Delta^{k}_{12}\mathcal{A}\right)^{j^{\prime}}_{j^{\prime}}(0)\right|^{2}\leq\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\nu+1}}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s_{0}}\left|\left(\Delta_{12}^{k}\mathcal{A}\right)^{j^{\prime}}_{j}(l)\right|^{2}.

Therefore, combining the above two inequalities, we obtain
|\Delta^{k}_{12}r(j)|\leq_{s_{0}}\mathfrak{M}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s_{0})\leq\mathfrak{M}^{\sharp,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(0,s_{0})
, which proves the desired estimate, taking the supremum in
\omega,\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
.
∎

2.3
Modified fractional Laplacians:
\Lambda^{\alpha-1}
and
\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}
.

For
f\in C^{\infty}_{0}
, we define

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\alpha-1}f(x):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos(x-y))^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(f(x)-f(y))dy,\quad\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}f(x):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos(x-y))^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}f(y)dy.

(2.67)

We denote the multipliers of
\Lambda^{\alpha-1}
and
\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}
by

\displaystyle m^{\circ}_{1,\alpha}(j):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos y)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(1-e^{-\text{i}jy})dy,\quad m^{\circ}_{2,\alpha}(j):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos y)^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}e^{-\text{i}jy}dy.

(2.68)

More explicitly, We have that
[
26
, Lemma 2.6 and Eq. (2.1)]

\displaystyle\displaystyle m^{\circ}_{1,\alpha}(j):

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(|j|+\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1+|j|-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right),\quad\displaystyle m_{2,\alpha}^{\circ}(j):=2\pi\frac{(-1)^{j}\Gamma(3-\alpha)}{\Gamma(2+|j|-\frac{\alpha}{2})\Gamma(2-|j|-\frac{\alpha}{2})},

(2.69)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}:=-\frac{2\pi\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}>0.

(2.70)

With
m^{\circ}_{1,\alpha},m^{\circ}_{2,\alpha}
above, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\alpha-1}f(x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}{m^{\circ}_{1,\alpha}}(j)\widehat{f}_{j}e^{\text{i}jx},\quad\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}f(x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}m^{\circ}_{2,\alpha}(j)\widehat{f}_{j}e^{\text{i}jx}.

(2.71)

One can show that using the asymptotic expansion of Gamma functions and Euler’s reflection formula
[
100
, Theorem 2.1]
that for
\alpha\in(0,2)\backslash\left\{1\right\}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{|j|\to\infty}m^{\circ}_{1,\alpha}(j)|j|^{-(\alpha-1)}=C_{1,\alpha},\quad\lim_{|j|\to\infty}m^{\circ}_{2,\alpha}(j)|j|^{-(\alpha-3)}=C_{2,\alpha},

(2.72)

for some constants
C_{1,\alpha},C_{2,\alpha}
.

We will also consider the weighted operators:

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\alpha-1}_{a}h:=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos(x-y))^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}a(x,y)(h(x)-h(y))dy,

(2.73)

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}_{a}h:=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos(x-y))^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}a(x,y)h(y)dy.

Therefore defining for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{\Lambda}(x,j)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos y)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}a(x,x-y)(1-e^{-\text{i}jy})dy,

(2.74)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{\Upsilon}(x,j)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos y)^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}a(x,x-y)e^{-\text{i}jy}dy,

we have that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{a}^{\alpha-1}h(x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{a}_{\Lambda}(x,j)\widehat{h}_{j}e^{\text{i}jx},\quad\Upsilon_{a}^{\alpha-3}h(x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{a}_{\Upsilon}(x,j)\widehat{h}_{j}e^{\text{i}jx}.

(2.75)

In the rest of this subsection, we aim to find smooth symbols to represent the operators
\Lambda^{\alpha-1},\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}
, In order to use the lemmas introduced in Subsection
2.2
. More precisely, we will extend the above symbols to be defined on
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
.

2.3.0.1
Analysis of
\Lambda^{\alpha-1}
:

We study the operator
\Lambda^{\alpha-1}
. We mainly focus on the properties of the multiplier:

\displaystyle\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}(j):=jm^{\circ}_{1,\alpha}(j),\text{ for $j\in\mathbb{Z}$.}

(2.76)

Indeed, (
2.71
) tells us that the operator
\partial_{x}\Lambda^{\alpha-1}
can be characterized by the symbol
i\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}(j)
.

Let us denote

\displaystyle\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha-1}(j):=\frac{\Gamma(|j|+\frac{\alpha}{2})}{\Gamma(1+|j|-\frac{\alpha}{2})},

(2.77)

so that we can write
\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}
as, recalling
m^{\circ}_{1,\alpha}
from (
2.69
),

\displaystyle\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}(j)=\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}j(\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha-1}(j)-\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha-1}(0))=\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}j\left(\frac{\Gamma(|j|+\frac{\alpha}{2})}{\Gamma(1+|j|-\frac{\alpha}{2})}-\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})}{\Gamma(1-\frac{\alpha}{2})}\right).

(2.78)

Note that
\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha-1}
is defined only on the integers. Recall that the function
z\mapsto\Gamma(z)
is holomorphic on
\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\text{Re}(z)>0\right\}
. Since we always assume that
\alpha\in(1,2)
,
\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha-1}
can be naturally extended to a smooth function on
\mathbb{R}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
as:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi):=\frac{\Gamma(|\xi|+\frac{\alpha}{2})}{\Gamma(1+|\xi|-\frac{\alpha}{2})}\text{ for $\xi\in\mathbb{R}$.}

(2.79)

Lemma 2.30
.

For each
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R},|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)\langle\xi\rangle^{-(\alpha-1)+\eta}\leq_{\alpha,\eta}1.

(2.80)

Proof.

We prove it by usual induction. For
\eta=0
, we recall Gautschi’s inequality
[
101
, Remark 2.1.1]
:

\displaystyle x^{1-s}<\frac{\Gamma(x+1)}{\Gamma(x+s)}<(x+s)^{1-s},\text{ for all $x>0$ and $s\in(0,1)$.}

(2.81)

Plugging
x=\frac{\alpha}{2}-1+|\xi|>0
for
|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2}
and
s=(2-\alpha)\in(0,1)
, we have that

\displaystyle\left(|\xi|-\frac{2-\alpha}{2}\right)^{\alpha-1}<\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)<\left(|\xi|+\frac{2-\alpha}{2}\right)^{\alpha-1},\text{ for all $\xi\in\mathbb{R}$},

(2.82)

This proves (
2.80
) when
\eta=0
.

Now we assume that (
2.80
) holds true for some
\eta\geq 0
and aim to prove it for
\partial_{\xi}^{\eta+1}\lambda_{\alpha-1}
. Since
\xi\mapsto\lambda_{\alpha-1}
is even, let us assume, without loss of generality, that
\xi>0
to obtain (
2.80
). We denote by
\psi^{(\eta)}(\xi)
be the
\eta
-th polygamma function:

\displaystyle\psi^{(\eta)}(\xi):=-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t^{\xi-1}}{1-t}(\log{t})^{\eta}dt,\quad\psi^{(0)}(\xi):=\log\xi-\frac{1}{2\xi}-2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t}{(t^{2}+\xi^{2})(e^{2\pi t}-1)}dt,\text{ for $\eta\in\mathbb{N}$, $0<\xi\in\mathbb{R}$}.

(2.83)

We collect necessary properties of the polygamma functions
[
73
, Lemma 1]
:

\displaystyle\psi^{(0)}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\xi)}{\Gamma(\xi)}\text{ for all $\xi>0$.}

(2.84)

\displaystyle\psi^{(\eta)}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{d}{d\xi}\right)^{\eta}\psi^{(0)}(\xi),\text{ for $\eta,\xi\geq 0$.}

(2.85)

\displaystyle\frac{(\eta-1)!}{\xi^{\eta}}+\frac{\eta!}{2\xi^{\eta+1}}

\displaystyle\leq(-1)^{\eta+1}\psi^{(\eta)}(\xi)\leq\frac{(\eta-1)!}{\xi^{\eta}}+\frac{\eta!}{\xi^{\eta+1}},\text{ for $\eta\geq 1$, $\xi>0$.}

(2.86)

From (
2.86
) and the definition of
\psi^{(0)}(\xi)
in (
2.83
), it is clear that

\displaystyle|\psi^{(\eta)}(\xi+\alpha/2)-\psi^{(\eta)}(\xi+1-\alpha/2)|\leq_{\eta}\langle\xi\rangle^{-\eta-1},\text{ for $\xi\geq\frac{1}{2}$ and $\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$.}

(2.87)

Also, using (
2.84
) and
\lambda_{\alpha-1}
in (
2.79
), we have that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\xi}\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)=\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)(\psi^{(0)}(\xi+\frac{\alpha}{2})-\psi^{(0)}(\xi+1-\frac{\alpha}{2})),\text{ for $\xi>0$}.

(2.88)

Therefore, it is straightforward that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{d}{d\xi}\right)^{\eta+1}\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\frac{d}{d\xi}\right)^{\eta}\left(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)(\psi^{(0)}(\xi+\frac{\alpha}{2})-\psi^{(0)}(\xi+1-\frac{\alpha}{2}))\right)\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{poly3}}{=}\left|\sum_{k=0}^{\eta}\binom{\eta}{k}\left(\left(\frac{d}{d\xi}\right)^{k}(\lambda_{\alpha-1})(\xi)(\psi^{(\eta-k)}(\xi+\alpha/2)-\psi^{(\eta-k)}(\xi+1-\alpha/2))\right)\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{cartmanandbunny}}{\leq}_{\eta}\sum_{k=0}^{\eta}\binom{\eta}{k}\left|\left(\frac{d}{d\xi}\right)^{k}(\lambda_{\alpha-1})(\xi)\right|\langle\xi\rangle^{-(\eta-k+1)}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{kyleandcartman12}}{\leq}_{\eta}\sum_{k=0}^{\eta}\binom{\eta}{k}\langle\xi\rangle^{(\alpha-1)-k}\langle\xi\rangle^{-(\eta-k+1)}

\displaystyle\leq_{\eta}\langle\xi\rangle^{(\alpha-1)-(\eta+1)},

which gives us (
2.80
) for
\partial_{\xi}^{(\eta+1)}\lambda_{\alpha-1}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 2.31
.

For
\xi\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
\xi\mapsto\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)
is strictly monotone-increasing and

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)\geq_{\alpha}\xi^{\alpha-2},\quad\text{ for $\xi\geq\frac{1}{2}$}.

(2.89)

Proof.

From (
2.82
), we have that
\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)\geq_{\alpha}\xi^{\alpha-1}
for
\xi>\frac{1}{2}
. Also, from (
2.86
), we have
\psi^{(1)}(\xi)\geq\frac{1}{\xi}+\frac{1}{2\xi^{2}}
for
\xi>\frac{1}{2}
, especially (since
\alpha>1
) (
2.85
) and the mean-value theorem tells us that

\displaystyle\psi^{(0)}(\xi+\frac{\alpha}{2})-\psi^{(0)}(\xi+1-\frac{\alpha}{2})\geq_{\alpha}\frac{1}{\xi}.

Therefore, the result follows from (
2.88
).
∎

Now, we study the multipliers
\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}
and
\lambda_{\alpha-1}
at integer points.

Lemma 2.32
.

For all
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
such that
j\neq k
, it holds that

\displaystyle|\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}(j)-\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}(k)|\geq_{\alpha}|j-k|\left(|j|^{\alpha-1}+|k|^{\alpha-1}\right).

(2.90)

Proof.

Note that
j\mapsto\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}(j)
is odd. Therefore, without loss of generality, we only need to prove (
2.90
) for the cases:
j>0>k
and
j>k>0
.

Proof for
j>0>k
.

We first observe from
\psi^{(0)}
in (
2.83
) that
\xi\mapsto\psi^{(0)}(\xi)
is strictly increasing for
\xi>0
. Since
\Gamma(\xi)
is strictly positive for
\xi>0
, we have
\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)>0
. Especially, (
2.88
) tells us that
\lambda_{\alpha-1}
is strictly increasing function for
\xi>0
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)>\lambda_{\alpha-1}(0)>0,\text{ for all $\xi\geq 0$.}

(2.91)

Thus, for
j>0>k
, it follows from (
2.78
) that

\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}(j)-\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}(k)\geq\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\left(j\left(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(0)\right)+|k|\left(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(0)\right)\right)\geq_{\alpha}j^{\alpha}+|k|^{\alpha},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma (
2.31
). The above inequality certainly implies (
2.90
).

Proof for
j>k>0
.

It follows from (
2.91
) and

\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}(j)-\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}(k)=\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\int_{k}^{j}\left(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(0)\right)+\xi\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)d\xi\geq\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\int_{k}^{j}\xi\lambda_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)d\xi.

Using Lemma
2.31
, we have

\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}(j)-\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}(k)\geq_{\alpha}j^{\alpha}-k^{\alpha},

which implies (
2.90
).
∎

Lemma 2.33
.

For all
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
such that
j+k\neq 0
, it holds that

|\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}(j+k)-\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}(j)-\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}(k)|\geq_{\alpha}(\max\left\{|j|,|k|\right\})^{\alpha-1}\min\left\{|j|,|k|\right\}.

Proof.

In view of (
2.78
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle|(j+k)\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j+k)-j\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j)-k\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)|\geq_{\alpha}(\max\left\{|j|,|k|\right\})^{\alpha-1}\min\left\{|j|,|k|\right\}.

(2.92)

Furthermore, we will assume, without loss of generality, that
j>0
. Under this assumption, we consider three cases:
j=k>0,\ j>k>0,\ k>j>0
, and
j>0>k
.

Case
j=k>0
:
In this case, we have

\displaystyle|(j+k)\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j+k)-j\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j)-k\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)|=2k(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(2k)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)).

(2.93)

If
k=1
, then it follows from Lemma
2.31
that

\displaystyle 2k(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(2k)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k))\geq_{\alpha}1=k^{\alpha}.

(2.94)

If
k\geq 2
, then, using (
2.82
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\alpha-1}(2k)

\displaystyle>\left(2k-\frac{2-\alpha}{2}\right)^{\alpha-1}>k^{\alpha-1}\left(2-\frac{2-\alpha}{2k}\right)^{\alpha-1}\overset{k\geq 2}{>}k^{\alpha-1}\left(\frac{7}{4}\right)^{\alpha-1},

\displaystyle\quad\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)

\displaystyle<\left(k+\frac{2-\alpha}{2}\right)^{\alpha-1}\leq k^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\frac{(2-\alpha)}{2k}\right)^{\alpha-1}\overset{k\geq 2}{\leq}k^{\alpha-1}\left(\frac{5}{4}\right)^{\alpha-1}.

Hence, it follows that

\lambda_{\alpha-1}(2k)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)>_{\alpha}k^{\alpha-1}.

Plugging this and (
2.94
) into (
2.93
), we get

\displaystyle|(j+k)\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j+k)-j\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j)-k\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)|>_{\alpha}k^{\alpha},\text{ if $k=j>0$},

(2.95)

which gives (
2.92
).

Case
j>k>0
:
Using Lemma
2.31
, we compute

\displaystyle|(j+k)\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j+k)-j\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j)-k\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)|

\displaystyle=j\left(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j+k)-\lambda_{\alpha}(j)\right)+k\left(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(j+k)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(k)\right)

\displaystyle>_{\alpha}j\int_{j}^{j+k}\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)d\xi

\displaystyle>_{\alpha}j|j+k|^{\alpha-2}k.

(2.96)

Then, we can further compute

\displaystyle j|j+k|^{\alpha-2}k\geq_{\alpha}\begin{cases}jk^{\alpha-1}\geq_{\alpha}j^{\alpha-1}k,&\text{ if $k<j\leq 2k$,}\\
jj^{\alpha-2}k\geq_{\alpha}j^{\alpha-1}k,&\text{ if $j>2k$.}\end{cases}

Plugging this into (
2.3.0.1
), we obtain (
2.92
).

Case
k>j>0
:
Changing the role of
j,k
in the case where
j>k>0
above, we get (
2.92
).

Case
j>0>k
:
In this case, we replace
k
by
-k
, then (
2.92
) follows from the above cases.
∎

Finally we summarize the results for the multipliers
m_{1,\alpha}
and
\lambda_{\alpha}
, which are proper extensions of
m_{1,\alpha}^{\circ}
and
\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}
to
\mathbb{R}
.

Proposition 2.34
.

There exist real-valued classical symbols
\lambda_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha},m_{1,\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha-1}
such that

(1)

It holds that
and
\Lambda^{\alpha-1}=Op^{W}(m_{1,\alpha})
and
\partial_{x}\Lambda^{\alpha-1}=Op^{W}(\text{i}\lambda_{\alpha})
as linear operators on
C^{\infty}_{0}
(set of smooth periodic functions with zero-average. See (
2.1
)). That is, it holds that

\displaystyle m_{1,\alpha}(j)=m_{1,\alpha}^{\circ}(j)=\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(|j|+\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1+|j|-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right),\text{ for all $j\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}$,}

(2.97)

and

\displaystyle\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi)=\xi m_{1,\alpha}(\xi),\text{ for all $\xi\in\mathbb{R}$},

(2.98)

(2)

\xi\mapsto m_{1,\alpha}(\xi)
is strictly positive, even and monotone increasing.

(3)

For all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-1}\leq_{\alpha}|\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi)|\leq_{\alpha}\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-1}
, therefore
\frac{1}{\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha}}\in\mathcal{S}^{1-\alpha}
.

(4)

For all
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
such that
j+k\neq 0
, it holds that

|\lambda_{\alpha}(j+k)-\lambda_{\alpha}(j)-\lambda_{\alpha}(k)|\geq_{\alpha,k}|j|^{\alpha-1}.

(5)

For all
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
such that
j\neq k
, it holds that

\displaystyle|j-k|\left(|j|^{\alpha-1}+|k|^{\alpha-1}\right)\leq_{\alpha}|\lambda_{\alpha}(j)-\lambda_{\alpha}(k)|\leq_{\alpha}|j-k|\left(|j|^{\alpha-1}+|k|^{\alpha-1}\right).

(2.99)

Proof.

We recall from (
2.79
) that
\lambda_{\alpha-1}
is well-defined for all real numbers and smooth everywhere except for
0
. In view of (
2.69
), we define
m_{1,\alpha}
on
\mathbb{R}
so that
m_{1,\alpha}(\xi)
is strictly positive, even, smooth on
\mathbb{R}
, and monotone increasing for
0<\xi<\frac{1}{2}
and

m_{1,\alpha}(\xi):=\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\left(\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(0)\right),\text{ if $|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2}$,}

and even and smooth in
\mathbb{R}
. Such an extension is possible since
\lambda_{\alpha-1}
is even, smooth, strictly increasing and
\lambda_{\alpha-1}(\xi)-\lambda_{\alpha-1}(0)>0
for
\xi\geq\frac{1}{2}
, and which follows from (
2.91
), Lemma
2.31
, Lemma
2.30
, and
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
defined in (
2.70
) is a strictly positive constant for each
\alpha\in(1,2)
. Once
m_{1,\alpha}(\xi)
is defined for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, we define
\lambda_{\alpha}:=\xi m_{1,\alpha}
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
.

By its definition, we have
m_{1,\alpha}=m_{1,\alpha}^{\circ}
and
\lambda_{\alpha}=\lambda_{\alpha}^{\circ}
on
\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
, therefore we have
Op^{W}(m_{1,\alpha})=\Lambda^{\alpha-1}
and
Op^{W}(\text{i}\xi\lambda_{\alpha})=\partial_{x}\Lambda^{\alpha-1}
on
C^{\infty}_{0}
. Thanks to Lemma
2.30
, we have that
m_{1,\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha-1}
and
\lambda_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
are classical symbols. Threfore
(1)
and
(2)
follow. Since
m_{1,\alpha}
is strictly positive and monotone increasing,
\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi)=m_{1,\alpha}(\xi)+\xi\partial_{\xi}m_{1,\alpha}(\xi)>0
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore,
(3)
follows from Lemma
2.31
. Again, since
\lambda_{\alpha}=\lambda^{\circ}_{\alpha}
on
\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
,
(4)
and the lower bound in
(5)
follow from Lemma
2.32
and Lemma
2.33
, respectively.

Now, it suffices to prove the upper bound in (
2.99
). Using
\lambda_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
and assuming
j>k
, without loss of generality, we have

|\lambda_{\alpha}(j)-\lambda_{\alpha}(k)|=\left|\int_{k}^{j}\partial_{\xi}(\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi))d\xi\right|\leq_{\alpha}\left|\int_{k}^{j}|\xi|^{\alpha-1}d\xi\right|\leq_{\alpha}|j-k|(|j|^{\alpha-1}+|k|^{\alpha-1}),

which gives the upper bound in (
2.99
).
∎

Lemma 2.35
.

For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
, there exists a classical symbol
\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)\in\mathcal{S}^{1-\alpha}
such that

\displaystyle\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi)-\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi-k)-\lambda_{\alpha}(k)},\text{ for all $\xi\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0,k\right\}$ }.

(2.100)

Proof.

Thanks to
(4)
of Proposition
2.34
(replacing
j
by
-j
), we see that
\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)
is well defined for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
except for
\xi=0
and
\xi=k
for which the denominator vanishes. Therefore, we can construct
\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)
for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
in such a way that

\displaystyle\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi)-\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi-k)-\lambda_{\alpha}(k)},&\text{ if $|\xi-0|\geq\frac{1}{2}$ or $|\xi-k|\geq\frac{1}{2}$ },\\
0,&\text{ if $\xi=0$ or $\xi=k$},\end{cases}

and it is smooth in
\mathbb{R}
. In order to see
\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)\in\mathcal{S}^{1-\alpha}
, we only need to focus on large
\xi
, that is, it is sufficient to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{|\xi|\to\infty}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)\langle\xi\rangle^{-(1-\alpha)+\eta}\leq_{k,\eta,\alpha}1.

(2.101)

This follows straightforwardly from the usual induction argument, using that
\lambda_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
. Without loss of generality, let us assume that
k>0
. We will show

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\to\infty}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)\langle\xi\rangle^{-(1-\alpha)+\eta}\leq_{k,\eta,\alpha}1.

(2.102)

Note that the other necessary estimate
\sup_{\xi\to-\infty}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)\langle\xi\rangle^{-(1-\alpha)+\eta}\leq_{k,\eta}1
follows in the same way, hence we will omit the proof.

Towards (
2.102
), we see that for
\xi\gg 0
and
\eta=0
,

\displaystyle|\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)\langle\xi\rangle^{-(1-\alpha)}|=\left|\frac{\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-1}}{\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi)-\lambda_{\alpha}({\xi-k})-\lambda_{\alpha}(k)}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\left|\frac{\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi+(t-1)k)dt}{\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-1}}\right|-\frac{\lambda_{\alpha}(k)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-1}}}.

(2.103)

Since
\xi\mapsto m_{1,\alpha}
is monotone increasing for
\xi\geq 0
(
(2)
of Proposition
2.34
), we see from (
2.98
) that

\lim_{\xi\to\infty}\left|\frac{\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi+(t-1)k)dt}{\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-1}}\right|=\lim_{\xi\to\infty}\frac{\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi+(t-1)k)|dt}{\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-1}}\geq_{\alpha}1,

where the last inequality follows from
(3)
of Proposition
2.34
. Therefore, taking
\xi
to
\infty
in (
2.103
), we obtain (
2.102
), when
\eta=0
. For
\eta+1\geq 1
, we have that for
\xi\geq k+1
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta+1}\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)|

\displaystyle=|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}(|\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)|^{2}(\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi)-\partial_{\xi}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi-k)))|

\displaystyle=\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\left(|\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)|^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\xi}^{2}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi+(t-1)k)dt\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}=\eta}C_{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta_{1}}(\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)^{2})\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta_{2}+2}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi+(t-1)k)dt\right|.

Using the induction hypothesis (
2.102
), we have
|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta_{1}}(\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)^{2})|\leq_{\eta,\alpha,k}\langle\xi\rangle^{2(1-\alpha)-\eta_{1}}
, while
(3)
of Proposition
2.34
gives us that
|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta_{2}+2}\lambda_{\alpha}(\xi+(t-1)k)|\leq_{\eta,k,\alpha}\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-\eta_{2}-2}
. Therefore, we have that

|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta+1}\kappa_{k,1-\alpha}(\xi)|\leq_{\eta,\alpha,k}\langle\xi\rangle^{-\alpha-\eta}=\langle\xi\rangle^{(1-\alpha)-(\eta+1)}.

This proves (
2.102
) for
\eta+1
.
∎

2.3.0.2
Analysis of
\Upsilon_{a}^{\alpha-3}

We study the operator
\Upsilon_{a}^{\alpha-3}
defined in (
2.73
), for some
a=a(\omega,\varphi,x,y)
and
\omega\in\Omega
and
(\varphi,x)\in\mathbb{T}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{T}
. Especially, we aim to express the operator
\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}_{a}
as a pseudo differential operator with a classical symbol.

Using (
2.12
) and (
2.73
), it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}_{a}=Op(\mathfrak{a}),\text{ where }\mathfrak{a}(\omega,\varphi,x,j):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos y)^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}a(\omega,\varphi,x,x-y)e^{-\text{i}jy}dy\text{ for $j\in\mathbb{Z}$}.

(2.104)

Here, the
“symbol”
,
\mathfrak{a}(\omega,\varphi,x,j),
is defined only for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. In order to make a use of the lemmas studied in Subsection
2.2
, we aim to find an extension
\mathfrak{a}(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)
, which is well-defined for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, so that the symbol can be measured by the norms in (
2.34
). Of course, a particular choice of an extension does not change the operator, since our pseudo differential operators that we consider in this paper always act on periodic functions.

To this end, we pick
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
to be smooth non-negative bump functions on
\mathbb{R}
such that
\text{supp}(\psi_{1})\in(-1/4,1/4)
with
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{1}(\xi)d\xi=1
, and
\text{supp}(\psi_{2})\in(-\pi/2,\pi/2)
with
\psi_{2}(y)=1
for
y\in(-\pi/3,\pi/3)
. For such
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
, we define
S_{\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)
as

\displaystyle S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(2-2\cos y)^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}a(\omega,\varphi,x,x-y)\psi_{2}(y)e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy,

(2.105)

\displaystyle S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(2-2\cos y)^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}a(\omega,\varphi,x,x-y)(1-\psi_{2}(y))e^{-\text{i}jy}dy,

(2.106)

\displaystyle\text{ for a unique $j\in\mathbb{Z}$ such that $\xi\in(j-1/2,j+1/2]$},

\displaystyle S_{2,\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\psi_{1}*S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{1}(\xi-\xi_{0})S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi_{0})d\xi_{0},

(2.107)

\displaystyle S_{\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)

\displaystyle:=S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi)+S_{2,\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,\xi).

(2.108)

Note that
S_{\Upsilon}(a)
is smooth in
\varphi,x,\xi
, if so is
a
, and

S_{\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,j)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(2-2\cos y)^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}a(\omega,\varphi,x,x-y)e^{-\text{i}jy}dy,

when
\xi=j
for some
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. Therefore, (
2.104
) tells us that

\displaystyle Op(S_{\Upsilon}(a))=\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}_{a}.

(2.109)

Now, we aim to prove that
S_{\Upsilon}(a)
is a classical symbol in
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha-3}
, that is,
\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}(S_{\Upsilon}(a))\langle\xi\rangle^{-(\alpha-3)+\eta}
is bounded for each
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We will consider
S_{1,\Upsilon}
and
S_{2,\Upsilon}
in (
2.108
) separately.

We first analyze the symbol
S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)
.

Lemma 2.36
.

For each
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists
\mu=\mu(\eta,\nu)\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle|S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{\alpha-3,s,\eta}\leq_{s,\eta,\alpha,\nu}\rVert a\rVert^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{H^{s+\mu}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})},\text{ for all $s\geq s_{0}$.}

(2.110)

Proof.

Let
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
s\geq s_{0}
be fixed, and let us choose
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
s_{1}+s_{2}=s
. For
\omega,\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\Omega
, we denote

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}^{k}a(\varphi,x,y)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}a(\omega,\varphi,x,y),&\text{ if $k=0$,}\\
\gamma\frac{a(\omega_{1},\varphi,x,y)-a(\omega_{2},\varphi,x,y)}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|},&\text{ if $k=1$},\end{cases}

(2.111)

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}^{k}S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)(\varphi,x,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)(\omega,\varphi,x,y),&\text{ if $k=0$,}\\
\gamma\frac{S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)(\omega_{1},\varphi,x,\xi)-S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)(\omega_{2},\varphi,x,\xi)}{|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|},&\text{ if $k=1$}.\end{cases}

(2.112)

Since
a\mapsto S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)
is linear, we have that

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}^{k}(\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}^{s_{2}}S_{1,\Upsilon}(a))(\varphi,x,\xi)=S_{1,\Upsilon}(\Delta_{12}^{k}(\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}^{s_{2}}a))(\varphi,x,\xi),

(2.113)

while (
2.105
) tells us that

\displaystyle S_{1,\Upsilon}(\Delta_{12}^{k}(\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}^{s_{2}}a))(\varphi,x,\xi)=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}|y|^{2-\alpha}\underbrace{\left(\frac{2-2\cos y}{|y|^{2}}\right)^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\psi_{2}(y)\Delta^{k}_{12}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}^{s_{2}}(a(\varphi,x,x-y))}_{=:f(\varphi,x,y)}e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy.

(2.114)

Since
\text{supp}(\psi_{2})\subset(-\pi/2,\pi/2)
, it holds that
\text{supp}(f(\varphi,x,\cdot))\subset(-\pi/2,\pi/2)
. Therefore, applying Lemma
2.37
, which will be proved after the proof of this Lemma, we get

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}(S_{1,\Upsilon}(\Delta_{12}^{k}(\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}^{s_{2}}a)))(\varphi,x,\xi)\right|\leq_{\alpha,\eta}\rVert f(\varphi,x,\cdot)\rVert_{H^{\eta+3}(\mathbb{T})}\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-3-\eta}.

(2.115)

From the definition of
f
in (
2.114
), it is clear that (using the usual Sobolev embedding theorem) we can choose
\mu(\nu)>0
such that

\displaystyle\rVert f(\varphi,x,\cdot)\rVert_{H^{\eta+3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq_{s,\nu}\rVert a\rVert_{H^{s+\mu(\nu)+\eta+3}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}.

(2.116)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\Delta^{k}_{12}(S_{1,\Upsilon}(a))(\cdot,\cdot,\xi)\rVert_{s}

\displaystyle\leq_{s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1}+s_{2}=s,\\
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\end{subarray}}\rVert\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}^{s_{2}}S_{1,\Upsilon}(a)\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varphi,x}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{rjjsdsd1sdsd}}{=}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1}+s_{2}=s,\\
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\end{subarray}}\rVert\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}S_{1,\Upsilon}(\Delta_{12}^{k}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}^{s_{2}}a)\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varphi,x}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{whattobys},\eqref{fsjsdpwosmds}}{\leq_{s,\alpha,\eta,\nu}}\rVert a\rVert_{H^{s+\mu(\nu)+\eta+3}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha-3-\eta}.

Therefore, replacing
\mu(\nu)+\eta+3
by
\mu(\nu,\eta)
, we obtain (
2.110
).
∎

Lemma 2.37
.

Let
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{-\pi,\pi})
be such that
\text{supp}(f)\subset(-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2})
. We denote

\mathfrak{b}(\xi):=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}|y|^{2-\alpha}f(y)e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy,\text{ for $(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}$.}

Then, for each
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathfrak{b}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}|\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}{\mathfrak{b}}(\xi)|\langle\xi\rangle^{-(\alpha-3)+\eta}\leq_{\alpha,\eta}\rVert f\rVert_{H^{\eta+3}(\mathbb{T})},\text{ for all $\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$.}

(2.117)

Proof.

Since (
2.117
) concerns only large
|\xi|
, we will assume that
|\xi|\geq 3
. We also assume that
\xi>0
, since the case where
\xi<0
can be done in the same manner. We argue by induction.

Proof for
\eta=0
.
For
\eta=0
, the integration by parts gives us that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\text{i}\xi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}((2-\alpha)y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y)+|y|^{2-\alpha}f^{\prime}(y)e^{-\text{i}\xi y})dy

\displaystyle=-\frac{\text{i}}{\xi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(2-\alpha)y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y)e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy-\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{d}{dy}(|y|^{2-\alpha}f^{\prime}(y))e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy

\displaystyle=:-\frac{\text{i}}{\xi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(2-\alpha)y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y)e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy+\mathfrak{b}_{1}(\xi).

Since
\alpha\in(1,2)
, the second integral already satisfies (
2.117
), that is

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathfrak{b}_{1}(\xi)|\langle\xi\rangle^{-(\alpha-3)}\leq\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\langle\xi\rangle^{1-\alpha}\rVert f\rVert_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\rVert f\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}.

For the first integral, using the change of variables (
\xi y\to y
), we see that

\displaystyle\frac{\text{i}}{\xi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y)e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy=\text{i}|\xi|^{\alpha}\xi^{-3}\int_{-\xi\pi}^{\xi\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy.

(2.118)

We choose
j\in\mathbb{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
\xi\in(j-1/2,j+1/2]
. Then, we can write the integral above as

\displaystyle\int_{-\xi\pi}^{\xi\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy=\int_{\pi}^{j\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy+\int_{-j\pi}^{-\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy+\mathfrak{b}_{2}(\xi),

where
\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathfrak{b}_{2}(\xi)|\leq C\rVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}
for some
C>0
. In view of (
2.118
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\pi}^{j\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy+\int_{-j\pi}^{-\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy\right|\leq_{\alpha}\rVert f\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}.

(2.119)

To this end, we write the first integral as

\displaystyle\int_{\pi}^{j\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi}^{j\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)\frac{1}{-\text{i}}\frac{d}{dy}(e^{-\text{i}y})dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\text{i}}\int_{\pi}^{j\pi}\left((1-\alpha)y^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)+\frac{1}{\xi}y^{1-\alpha}f^{\prime}(y/\xi)\right)e^{-\text{i}y}dy+(y^{1-\alpha}f(y/\xi)\frac{1}{-\text{i}}\bigg|_{y=\pi}^{y=j\pi},

therefore,

\left|\int_{\pi}^{j\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy\right|\leq_{\alpha}\left(\rVert f\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\frac{j}{|\xi|}\right)\leq C\rVert f\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})},

where the last inequality follows from
\xi\in(j-1/2,j+1/2]
and the Sobolev embedding theorem.
Similarly, we obtain
\left|\int_{-j\pi}^{-\pi}y|y|^{-\alpha}f(y/\xi)e^{-\text{i}y}dy\right|\leq_{\alpha}\rVert f\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}
which gives (
2.119
).

Proof for
\eta\geq 1
.
For
\eta\geq 1
, integration by parts gives us that

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}^{\eta}\mathfrak{b}(\xi)=-\frac{\text{i}}{\xi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(2-\alpha+\eta)|y|^{2-\alpha}y^{\eta-1}f(y)+|y|^{2-\alpha}y^{\eta-1}(yf^{\prime}(y))e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy.

(2.120)

Using the induction hypothesis, we have that

\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(2-\alpha+\eta)|y|^{2-\alpha}y^{\eta-1}f(y)+|y|^{2-\alpha}y^{\eta-1}(yf^{\prime}(y))e^{-\text{i}\xi y}dy\right|\langle\xi\rangle^{-(\alpha-3)+\eta-1}\leq_{\alpha,\eta}\rVert f\rVert_{H^{\eta+3}(\mathbb{T})}.

Taking account of the factor
\xi^{-1}
in (
2.120
), this proves the desired estimate (
2.117
).
∎

Now, we turn to
S_{2,\Upsilon}(a)
in (
2.107
).

Lemma 2.38
.

For each
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists
\mu=\mu(\eta)\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle|S_{2,\Upsilon}|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{\alpha-3,s,\eta}\leq_{s,\eta,\alpha,\nu}\rVert a\rVert^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{H^{s+\mu}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})},\text{ for all $s\geq s_{0}$}.

Proof.

Let us fix
\xi
and let
j
be the unique integer such that
\xi\in(j-1/2,j+1/2]
. From (
2.106
), (
2.107
) and noting that
\xi\mapsto\psi_{1}(\xi)
is supported on
\xi\in(-1/4,1/4)
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\rVert S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a)(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot,j)\rVert_{s}^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}\leq_{\alpha,\nu,s}\rVert a\rVert^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{H^{s+\mu}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})}\langle j\rangle^{-\eta},\text{ for all $s\geq s_{0}$ and $\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$},

(2.121)

for some
\mu=\mu(\eta)>0
. To this end, we use the notation in (
2.111
). For
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
s_{1}+s_{2}=s
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{k}_{12}\partial_{x}^{s_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{2}}S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a)(\varphi,x,j)

\displaystyle=S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(\Delta^{k}_{12}\partial_{x}^{s_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{2}}a)=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\Delta^{k}_{12}f(\varphi,x,y)e^{-\text{i}jy}dy,

\displaystyle\Delta^{k}_{12}f(\varphi,x,y)

\displaystyle:=(2-2\cos y)^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(1-\psi_{1}(y))\partial_{x}^{s_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{2}}(\Delta^{k}_{12}a(\varphi,x,x-y)).

(2.122)

From this, we note that
\Delta^{k}_{12}\partial_{x}^{s_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{2}}S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a)
is the
j
-th Fourier coefficient of the smooth map
y\mapsto\Delta^{k}_{12}f(\varphi,x,y)
for each fixed
\varphi,x
, since
\psi_{2}(y)=1
for
y\in(-\pi/3,\pi/3)
and
\text{supp}(\psi_{2})\in(-\pi/2,\pi/2)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle|\Delta^{k}_{12}\partial_{x}^{s_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{2}}S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a)(\varphi,x,j)|

\displaystyle\leq\rVert\Delta^{k}_{12}f(\varphi,x,\cdot)\rVert_{H^{\eta}(\mathbb{T})}\langle j\rangle^{-\eta}\leq\rVert\Delta^{k}_{12}f\rVert_{H^{s+\mu(\nu)+\eta}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})}\langle j\rangle^{-\eta},

(2.123)

for some
\mu(\nu)>0
, where the last inequality follows from the usual Sobolev embedding theorem.
From (
2.122
), it is clear that

\rVert\Delta^{k}_{12}f\rVert_{H^{s+\mu(\nu)+\eta}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})}\leq_{\nu}\rVert\Delta^{k}_{12}a\rVert_{H^{s+\mu(\nu)+\eta}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})}\leq\rVert a\rVert^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{H^{s+\mu(\nu)+\eta}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})}.

Therefore, combining this with (
2.123
), we get

\displaystyle\rVert S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a)(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot,j)\rVert^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{s}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\omega,\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\Omega}\sum_{s_{1}+s_{2}=s}|\Delta^{k}_{12}\partial_{x}^{s_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{s_{2}}S_{\text{step},\Upsilon}(a)(\cdot,\cdot,j)|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+1})}

\displaystyle\leq_{s}\rVert a\rVert^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{H^{s+\mu(\nu)+\eta}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})}\langle j\rangle^{-\eta},

which proves (
2.121
).
∎

The following proposition follows straightforwardly from Lemma
2.36
, Lemma
2.38
, (
2.108
) and (
2.109
):

Lemma 2.39
.

Given a function
a=a(\omega,\varphi,x,y)
,
S_{\Upsilon}(a)
defined in (
2.108
) is a classical symbol in
S^{\alpha-3}
and it satisfies

Op(S_{\Upsilon}(a))=\Upsilon^{\alpha-3}_{a},

with the estimates

\displaystyle|S_{\Upsilon}(a)|^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{\alpha-3,s,\eta}\leq_{s,\eta,\alpha,\nu}\rVert a\rVert^{\text{Lip}(\gamma,\Omega)}_{H^{s+\mu}(\mathbb{T}^{\nu+2})},\text{ for all $s\geq s_{0}$.}

(2.124)

2.4
Hamiltonian structure in
L^{2}_{0}

Given a Hamiltonian
H:L^{2}_{0}\mapsto\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}
, we define the
L_{0}^{2}
-gradient of
H
at
f\in L^{2}_{0}
,
\nabla H(f)
, as the unique vector field such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}\nabla_{L^{2}_{0}}H(f)(x)g(x)dx=\frac{d}{dt}H(f+tg)\bigg|_{t=0}.

(2.125)

Similarly, we define the
L^{2}_{0}
